\section{How to read an entry: two kinds of rule}\label{sec:legend}
This catalog contains rules of two kinds, and they are not the same sort of object. Telling them apart is the first thing to know about any rule in it.
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0.3em}
\item \textbf{Generated rules} are \emph{this project's output}: what the generator derived from a decomposition. They are printed in the body text, in black, each under a bold label that names the \texttt{cata/*.tex} file it was copied from and carries whatever verdict \texttt{make validate} issued for it.
\item \textbf{Published rules} are \emph{someone else's result}, summarised under citation. Every one of them sits inside a block with a coloured bar down its left margin, repeated on every page the block spans, opening with the words \emph{Published explanation --- not this project's output}. Each rule inside carries its own label, and a published rule typeset as a fraction also carries the marker \textcolor{litbar}{\sffamily[published]} inside the display itself.
\end{itemize}
A published rule is typeset as a $\frac{\text{premises}}{\text{conclusion}}$ \textbf{only where the source file states that its translation into this notation is faithful}. Where that file says the translation is not faithful, the paper's own form is printed instead and the file's reasons are printed under it. Most published explanations of these constraints quantify their premises over an object that exists only at propagation time --- a Hall interval, a strongly connected component of a residual graph, the cut of a flow network, the set of tasks with a compulsory part at a time point --- and this notation has no index set that names one. Forcing such a rule into a fraction would state something about the literature that the literature does not say.
Where an entry has both kinds, a third block follows the pair, with a thin grey bar: the \emph{calibration}, the verdict relating the generated rule to the published one, on implication strength. Its vocabulary is below. A verdict in this document is never written as ``correct''.
\subsection{Provenance tags, as \texttt{catalog/\_literature/README.md} defines them}
Lifted from the file that defines them, in its own order. Each tag carries across unchanged wherever the claim it marks is printed; this document never upgrades one.
Every claim about a paper carries exactly one tag:

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.430\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.430\linewidth}@{}}
\hline
\textbf{tag} & \textbf{means} \\
\hline
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} & The document was fetched in this session and the formula/sentence is transcribed from it. The pointer (section, figure, page) resolves in the fetched document. \\
\provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} & Obtained by applying a substitution the paper states in prose to a formula the paper displays, and checked against the paper's own worked example. Not a memory reconstruction --- the file shows the substitution and the check. \\
\provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY} & Supported only by an abstract, a summary, or another author's description. The secondary source is named. \\
\provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} & Could not be obtained. The file says what was tried. \\
\hline
\end{tabular}\par}
There is no fifth tag. \textbf{A rule with no tag is a defect}, and so is a tag on a sentence whose source is ``the standard X explanation'' --- that is not a provenance.

\provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} marks a sentence that is \emph{this session's reading} of how the published rule relates to this repo's generated one. It is an opinion of C2's, not a claim about the paper, and a catalog entry that repeats it should say so.

\subsection{Calibration verdicts}
A separate axis from the status legend, which is in \S\ref{sec:howmade} with the rest of the status material: how a generated rule compares to the \emph{published} one, on implication strength. One of \texttt{agrees}, \texttt{weaker}, \texttt{stronger},
\texttt{incomparable}, \texttt{out of reach}, \texttt{no published rule}.
\subsection{How much of the literature is here}
\textbf{3} of the \textbf{121} entry files in \texttt{catalog/} carry a published explanation: \texttt{alldifferent}, \texttt{cumulative}, \texttt{gcc}. That is \textbf{3} of the \textbf{118} MiniZinc release globals.
\textbf{That is a count of what this repository has sourced, and nothing else.} It is not a claim that the literature on these three constraints is exhausted --- each file records what it could not obtain, in its own \emph{What was not sourced} section, printed here with the rest --- and it is not a claim about the other 115 release globals, whose literature, where any exists, is indexed in \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md} and is not reproduced in this document. The coverage sentence on the title page is about \emph{this method's} rules; it says nothing about the published ones.
\clearpage
\section{Entries with explanation rules}
Every entry that carries a rule of either kind: a \emph{generated} rule, produced by this project from a decomposition and copied verbatim from \texttt{cata/*.tex}, or a \emph{published} rule, someone else's result sourced into \texttt{catalog/\_literature/} and printed inside a coloured bar that says so. This document re-renders neither: a generated rule is copied, a published one is laid out as its source file records it. Where an entry has both, the calibration relating them follows the pair.

\rulebox{\frac{X_{i'}=t,~\forall i',~i' \neq i,~i' \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket}{X_{i} \neq t}}

\diag{generator diagnostics (W1-T3)\\ X\_\{i\}=t : 1 candidate(s) -> 0 rule(s); dropped F 1, cycle 0, duplicate 0, undefined index set 0\\   ** NO RULE EMITTED for X\_\{i\}=t: 1 candidate(s), all blocked **\\ X\_\{i\} \textbackslash{}neq t : 1 candidate(s) -> 1 rule(s); dropped F 0, cycle 0, duplicate 0, undefined index set 0}

\begin{pubblock}
\pubhead{Published explanation --- not this project's output}
{\small\sffamily Everything between this bar's ends is \textbf{someone else's published result}, summarised under citation in \texttt{catalog/\_\allowbreak{}literature/alldifferent.md} and reproduced here with that file's provenance tags carried across unchanged. Nothing inside was produced by the generator in this repository, and no tag is upgraded: \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} means the formula was transcribed from the fetched document, \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} that it was obtained by a substitution the paper states in prose, \provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY} that only a summary supports it, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} that it could not be obtained at all, and \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} that the sentence is the source file's own reading rather than a claim about the paper. Tags occurring in this file: \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}, \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED}, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.\par}
\litsub{Citation}
\textbf{Nicholas Downing, Thibaut Feydy, Peter J. Stuckey. \emph{Explaining alldifferent.} Proc. 35th Australasian Computer Science Conference (ACSC 2012), Melbourne, January-February 2012. CRPIT Vol. 122, Mark Reynolds and Bruce Thomas (eds.), Australian Computer Society.}

Read as the authors' preprint, \url{https://people.eng.unimelb.edu.au/pstuckey/papers/alldiff.pdf} (fetched 2026-09-21; 819.7 KB; the venue line above is transcribed from its own copyright footnote on preprint p.1). The preprint carries \textbf{no printed page numbers}, so every page pointer below is a \emph{preprint PDF page index}. CRPIT pagination not cross-checked. Repo index: \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:116}, \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:266}.

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
\textbf{Citation correction.} The task that commissioned this file attributed the Hall-set \texttt{alldifferent} explanation to \emph{Explaining flow-based propagation} (CPAIOR 2012, LNCS 7298:146-162). The Hall-set explanations are in \textbf{this} paper, the ACSC 2012 one. The CPAIOR paper does discuss \texttt{alldifferent}, but only as an instance of a \texttt{gcc} flow network (its Example 4); see \texttt{gcc.md}. \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:116} cites the ACSC paper correctly under the title \emph{Explaining alldifferent}, and \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:266} gives the ACSC venue. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (both papers fetched and compared).
\end{quotation}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{The paper's own vocabulary}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}3 ``Hall Sets'', preprint pp.2-3:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``Given a constraint \texttt{alldifferent(x1, .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}, xn)}, \texttt{H $\subseteq$ \{1,...,n\}} is a Hall set if \texttt{|H| $\geq$ |V|} where \texttt{V = $\cup$\_\{h$\in$H\} D(x\_h)}. If the inequality holds strictly, that is \texttt{|H| > |V|}, then the constraint is unsatisfiable. If it holds as an equality, \texttt{|H| = |V|}, then no variable \texttt{x\_i, i $\notin$ H} can take a value from \texttt{V}.''
\end{quotation}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}3, preprint p.3, on what the explanations are in general:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``The explanation clauses are essentially descriptions of the well-known conditions for pruning. Usually these clauses also suffice to describe failure (because they wake up implicit clauses requiring domains to be non-empty) but in some cases explicit failure nogoods can also be produced.''
\end{quotation}
\texttt{E} is defined in \S{}4 as \texttt{E = $\cup$\_\allowbreak{}\{i=1.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\} D\_\allowbreak{}orig(x\_\allowbreak{}i)}, the union of the \emph{original} domains.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 1 --- value-consistent (forward-checking) propagator}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}4 ``Global value-consistent propagator'', preprint p.3. The propagator ``wakes upon variable fixing, i.e. when \texttt{D(x\_h) = \{v\}} for some \texttt{h, v}, it prunes all \texttt{D(x\_i), i $\neq$ v} with explanation''

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x\_h = v] $\rightarrow$ [x\_i $\neq$ v].}
\par}
(The \texttt{i $\neq$ v} in that sentence is the paper's own typo for \texttt{i $\neq$ h}; the formula is unambiguous. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.)

\textbf{This repo's notation.} Faithful:

\rulekind{Published rule, translated into this repo's notation. The source file marks this translation faithful.}
\pubrulebox{\frac{X_h = t}{X_i \neq  t}\qquad (h \neq  i, h \in  \llbracket 1,n\rrbracket , i \in  \llbracket 1,n\rrbracket , AC)}
\textbf{Event explained.} A value removal, \texttt{[x\_i $\neq$ v]}. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Vocabulary.} User variables only. No auxiliaries. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} The paper does not claim minimality for this rule and does not need to: it is a single literal, so nothing can be dropped. The paper's strength discussion is empirical, not formal --- \S{}9 ``Discussion'', preprint p.7: ``the strong domain propagator produces a weaker nogood than value, since the nogood involves many variables and describes a situation that might not recur often enough to pay'' its cost. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. There is no formal minimality proof anywhere in the paper. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (by absence --- searched the preprint text for ``minimal''/``stronger''/``weaker''; the only ``minimal'' hit is ``minimal change'' to an algorithm, preprint p.3).

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Schema.} It is a fixed two-literal pattern over indices \texttt{h, i}, independent of any data structure. This is the one published \texttt{alldifferent} explanation a decomposition-derived generator can reach. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\textbf{Related.} \S{}4 also records, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, that when \texttt{|E| = n} the propagator additionally enforces the clauses \texttt{$\bigvee$\_\{i=1..n\} [x\_i = v]} for all \texttt{v $\in$ E}, which the paper says is ``equivalent to changing the upper bound of 1 to equality with 1 in the above linear constraints'' --- i.e. the counting-across-sums direction that this repo files under \textbf{E4} (\texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:116}).

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 2 --- bounds-consistent propagator (Hall interval)}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}5 ``Global bounds-consistent propagator'', preprint p.4, final display of the section. Given Hall set \texttt{H} with \texttt{V = a..b}, the increased lower bound for a variable \texttt{x\_i $\notin$ H} is explained as

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x\_i $\geq$ a] $\wedge$ $\bigwedge$\_\{h$\in$H\} ([x\_h $\geq$ a] $\wedge$ [x\_h $\leq$ b]) $\rightarrow$ [x\_i $\geq$ b + 1].}
\par}
followed by, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``This requires \texttt{O(n)} literals per explanation.''

\textbf{This repo's notation.} The clause body translates, but \textbf{the translation is not faithful as a schema}, and the reason matters:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the source file's attempted translation --- which that file marks \emph{not faithful}. Printed in the file's own layout, not as a rule of this catalog; the reasons follow.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_i $\geq$ a,~~X\_h $\geq$ a,~~X\_h $\leq$ b,~~$\forall$h $\in$ H}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ------------------------------- $\vdash$~~~~~~~~(BC)}
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_i $\geq$ b+1}
\par}
\texttt{H} here is \textbf{not} an index set the printer can name. It is a set discovered at run time by the union-find pass of Lopez-Ortiz et al. (2003) --- \S{}5, preprint p.4: the paper's only change to that algorithm was ``(ii) to collect the set \texttt{H}, required for explanations'' (\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}). Likewise \texttt{a} and \texttt{b} are the discovered interval endpoints, not indices drawn from \texttt{$\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$}. The repo's printer quantifies over declared index sets (\texttt{$\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$}, \texttt{$\llbracket$1,m$\rrbracket$}) and has no way to denote ``a set \texttt{H} such that \texttt{|H| = |a..b|}''. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\textbf{Event explained.} A bound change, \texttt{[x\_i $\geq$ b+1]}. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. (The paper notes, preprint p.4, that the algorithm as described ``will only prune lower bounds'' and that a second pass recomputes Hall intervals for upper bounds; the upper-bound rule is symmetric and is \textbf{not} displayed in the paper. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.)

\textbf{Vocabulary.} User variables only, with run-time constants \texttt{a, b} and a run-time set \texttt{H}. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} Not claimed. \S{}9 does argue these nogoods are reusable, preprint p.9: ``These nogoods are also symmetric between variables, since they describe a Hall interval rather than any specific pruning resulting from the existence of the Hall interval, which further promotes reuse.'' \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. That is a reuse argument, not a minimality claim.

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Per-propagation.} \texttt{H}, \texttt{a} and \texttt{b} come out of a union-find data structure built during the sweep. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (\S{}5's description of the algorithm).

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 3 --- domain-consistent propagator (R\'egin / SCC), pruning}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6 ``Global domain-consistent propagator'', preprint p.5, \textbf{equation (1)}. Given \texttt{H} and \texttt{V}, ``the explanation that we use for pruning values in \texttt{j $\in$ V} from \texttt{x\_i $\notin$ H} is''

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\bigwedge$\_\{h$\in$H, d$\in$E\textbackslash{}V\} [x\_h $\neq$ d] $\rightarrow$ [x\_i $\neq$ j]~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(1)}
\par}
with, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``It requires \texttt{|H|(|E| $-$ |V|)}, or \texttt{O(n|E|)} literals per explanation in the worst case.''

Note the \emph{polarity}: the premises are the values \textbf{outside} \texttt{V} that each \texttt{x\_h} has already lost --- the paper renders this as ``the list of dotted arcs leaving the SCC'' (\S{}6, preprint p.5). It is \textbf{not} \texttt{$\bigwedge$\_\{h$\in$H\} [x\_h $\in$ V]}; the paper tries that first and then says, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, of the failure case: ``Since we do not have literals to express that \texttt{x\_h $\in$ \{2,3\}}, we use an equivalent clausal representation \texttt{x\_h $\neq$ 1 $\wedge$ x\_h $\neq$ 4}.''

\textbf{This repo's notation.} Body translates; schema does not:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the source file's attempted translation --- which that file marks \emph{not faithful}. Printed in the file's own layout, not as a rule of this catalog; the reasons follow.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_h $\neq$ d,~~$\forall$h $\in$ H,~~$\forall$d $\in$ E\textbackslash{}V}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ------------------------------- $\vdash$~~~~~~~~(AC)}
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_i $\neq$ j}
\par}
\texttt{H} and \texttt{V} are the node sets of a strongly connected component of the residual graph of a bipartite matching. There is no index-set expression for them. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\textbf{Event explained.} A value removal. The same machinery also deduces \textbf{equalities} --- \S{}6, Example 6.4, preprint p.5, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``Pruning the arc from SCC \#2 $\rightarrow$ \#3 as described in Example 6.3 gives the residual graph of Figure 5c. Further pruning is possible: \texttt{x1} may be fixed to 1, using SCC \#3 as evidence, yielding explanation \texttt{[x1 $\neq$ 2] $\rightarrow$ [x1 = 1]}.'' The paper stresses this as its delta over prior work, \S{}6, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``Our explanations are the same as Katsirelos's (2008) except that, our explanations based on the list of dotted arcs leaving an SCC are quite general, so we naturally deduce and propagate equalities, rather than just disequalities as Katsirelos does.''

\textbf{Vocabulary.} User variables only. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} Not claimed. The paper says explanations are generated \emph{lazily} (\S{}6, preprint p.4, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``Note that we use SCC-splitting (Gent et al. 2008), and we generate explanations lazily'') and that redundant literals are stripped against the original domains (Example 6.2, preprint p.5, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``By removing the literals that are false in the original domains, we obtain the nogood \texttt{[x1 $\neq$ 1] $\wedge$ [x3 $\neq$ 4] $\rightarrow$ false}''). Stripping literals already false in \texttt{D\_orig} is a \emph{correctness/compaction} step, not a minimality proof.

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Per-propagation.} Tarjan's SCC algorithm on the residual graph of a Ford-Fulkerson matching produces \texttt{H} and \texttt{V}. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 4 --- failure}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. \S{}6, preprint p.4: ``For infeasibility, the set of nodes searched for an augmenting path (the cut) consists of \texttt{H $\cup$ V} and is a failure set as necessarily \texttt{|H| > |V|}.'' The resulting nogood in the worked example (Example 6.2, spanning preprint pp.4-5), after stripping:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x1 $\neq$ 1] $\wedge$ [x3 $\neq$ 4] $\rightarrow$ false}
\par}
and, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (preprint p.5): ``This final nogood is simply the list of dotted arcs leaving the cut.''

The general shape is equation (1) with \texttt{false} in place of \texttt{[x\_i $\neq$ j]}, \texttt{H $\cup$ V} the searched cut, and \texttt{|H| > |V|}. \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} --- the paper states the cut/dotted-arc correspondence in prose (\S{}6, preprint p.4) and displays only the instance; the generalisation is one substitution and matches the instance, but the paper does \textbf{not} display it. \textbf{Per-propagation}, same reason as rule 3.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 5 --- the \texttt{gcc}-style decomposition of \texttt{alldifferent} (Feydy \& Stuckey 2009)}
This is the one place the paper gives an explanation that arises from a \emph{decomposition} and is therefore directly comparable to this repo's method.

\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}7 ``alldifferent by decomposition'', preprint p.6. The decomposition, quoted verbatim from the paper's MiniZinc listing:

\rulekind{Quoted from the paper: the paper's own listing, not a rule.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small predicate alldifferent\_feydy\_decomp(}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~array[int] of var int: x) =}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~let \{ int: L = lb\_array(x),}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~~~~~int: C = ub\_array(x) + 1 - L,}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~~~~~int: N = length(x),}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~~~~~array[1..C] of var 0..1: c,}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~~~~~array[0..C] of var 0..N: s \} in}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~s[0] = 0 /\textbackslash{} s[C] = N /\textbackslash{}}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~forall (i in 1..C) (}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~s[i] = s[i - 1] + c[i] /\textbackslash{}}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~c[i] = sum (j in 1..N) (bool2int(x[j] = i)) /\textbackslash{}}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ~~~~s[i] = sum (j in 1..N) (bool2int(x[j] <= i)));}
\par}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``The new decomposition is efficient because it uses the literals \texttt{[x\_i = v]} and \texttt{[x\_i $\leq$ v]}, which are native in a lazy clause generation solver, as they are part of the integer variable encoding.''

\textbf{The explanation}, \S{}7 Example 7.1, preprint p.6, for \texttt{x1 $\in$ 1.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}2, x2 $\in$ 1.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}3, x3 $\in$ 2.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}3} with \texttt{x2} narrowed to \texttt{2..3}:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x2 $\geq$ 2] $\wedge$ [x3 $\geq$ 2] $\rightarrow$ [x1 $\leq$ 1]}
\par}
\textbf{This repo's notation.} Faithful for this instance:

\rulekind{Published rule, translated into this repo's notation. The source file marks this translation faithful.}
\pubrulebox{\frac{X_2 \geq  2,\; X_3 \geq  2}{X_1 \leq  1}\qquad (BC)}
\textbf{Event explained.} A bound change. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Vocabulary.} The \emph{conclusion and premises are over user variables}, but the derivation runs through the auxiliaries \texttt{c[i]} (occurrence counts) and \texttt{s[i]} (prefix sums of occurrences) --- the paper's text works the example through \texttt{s1 = $\Sigma$\_\allowbreak{}i bool2int([x\_\allowbreak{}i $\leq$ 1])}. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} Not claimed. The paper's characterisation of the reach of this decomposition is, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}7, preprint p.6: it gives ``the consistency level described in Section 4, plus the detection of Hall intervals aligned to the start or end of the domain interval \texttt{min(E)..max(E)}''. So: strictly between value-consistency and bounds-consistency.

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Schema} --- it is the explanation of a linear-sum propagator, which is exactly the kind of thing this repo's \texttt{rule5/6/7} schemas produce. But the sum is over \emph{integer} auxiliaries \texttt{s[i]} chained by \texttt{s[i] = s[i-1] + c[i]}, and the counting step \texttt{c[i] = $\Sigma$\_\allowbreak{}j bool2int(x[j] = i)} reasons across two different sums of the same Booleans. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}: that is this repo's \textbf{E1} (integer auxiliaries) plus \textbf{E4} (counting across sums); see \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:116}, which independently reaches the same conclusion.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Answers to the four catalog questions, condensed}
{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.172\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.172\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.172\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.172\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.172\linewidth}@{}}
\hline
 & \textbf{value-consistent (\S{}4)} & \textbf{bounds-consistent (\S{}5)} & \textbf{domain-consistent (\S{}6)} & \textbf{Feydy decomp (\S{}7)} \\
\hline
\textbf{Event} & value removal & lower-bound increase & value removal; also equality fixing; also failure & bound change \\
\textbf{Premises' vocabulary} & user vars, 1 literal & user vars over run-time set \texttt{H}, interval \texttt{a..b} & user vars over run-time \texttt{H}, \texttt{E\textbackslash{}V} & user vars; derivation via integer auxiliaries \texttt{c}, \texttt{s} \\
\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} & no formal claim anywhere in the paper; strength discussion is empirical (\S{}9) & idem; reuse argued, not minimality & idem; literals false in \texttt{D\_orig} are stripped, which is compaction not minimality & idem \\
\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} & \textbf{schema} & \textbf{per-propagation} (union-find) & \textbf{per-propagation} (matching + SCC) & \textbf{schema}, needs E1+E4 \\
\hline
\end{tabular}\par}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{What was not sourced \ \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}}
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item The upper-bound form of the bounds-consistent explanation. The paper states the symmetric case is ``analogous and omitted''. \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} (the paper does not give it).
\item CRPIT Vol. 122 page range for this paper. \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} --- the preprint has no printed page numbers and the CRPIT table of contents was not fetched (fetch budget).
\end{itemize}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{How the source file was produced}
{\small
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \texttt{WebFetch https:/\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}people.\allowbreak{}eng.\allowbreak{}unimelb.\allowbreak{}edu.\allowbreak{}au/\allowbreak{}pstuckey/\allowbreak{}papers/\allowbreak{}alldiff.\allowbreak{}pdf} (2026-09-21). The fetch tool's summariser could not read the PDF; it saved the binary, which was then run through \texttt{pdftotext -raw} and \texttt{pdftotext -layout} locally. \textbf{Every quote above is transcribed from that local text, not from the summariser.} \texttt{curl} to the same URL is blocked by the host's WAF (Incapsula), which is why WebFetch was used.
\item Page pointers: \texttt{pdftotext -\allowbreak{}raw -\allowbreak{}f <p> -\allowbreak{}l <p> alldiff.\allowbreak{}pdf -\allowbreak{} | grep -\allowbreak{}n '<phrase>'}, one page at a time, for every phrase cited. The preprint has 10 pages (\texttt{pdfinfo}).
\item The minimality claim is a \textbf{measurement}: \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}c -\allowbreak{}i 'minimal' <text>} over the whole preprint returns \textbf{1}, and that one hit is ``the original algorithm with minimal change'' (preprint p.3, about the union-find algorithm, not about an explanation). Section headings were enumerated with \texttt{grep -n '\textasciicircum{}[0-9] '}: \S{}1-\S{}9. (An earlier \texttt{grep -n '\textasciicircum{}[0-9] [A-Z]'} appeared to show no \S{}7; that was the pattern's fault --- \S{}7's heading is ``7 alldifferent by decomposition'', which begins lowercase. Corrected before committing.)
\item \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}o '\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}frac' cata/\allowbreak{}alldifferent.\allowbreak{}tex | wc -\allowbreak{}l} $\rightarrow$ \textbf{1} (run 2026-09-21), for the comparison section.
\item \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md} line numbers from \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n -\allowbreak{}i 'Downing' CHRISTMAS\_\allowbreak{}LIST.\allowbreak{}md}.

\par}
\end{pubblock}

\begin{calblock}
\calhead{Calibration --- the generated rule against the published one}
{\small\sffamily Verdict, from this entry's own header table: \textbf{weaker than published} (Downing et al. \S{}4); the \S{}5 and \S{}6 rules are \textbf{out of reach}.\par}
\litsubsub{From \texttt{catalog/alldifferent.md}, section ``Calibration (W3-T5, D-0013)''}
\textbf{Verdict: weaker than published.} Against \S{}4 --- the one published rule this method could have matched. \S{}5 and \S{}6 are \textbf{out of reach}, and \S{}7 is a near miss behind E1 + E4.

\textbf{The axis is implication strength} (the papers' own order; the minimality axis was retired when C2 measured that no paper claims it --- \texttt{docs/ROADMAP.md:99}).

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.287\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.287\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.287\linewidth}@{}}
\hline
 & \textbf{premise} & \textbf{conclusion} \\
\hline
published, \S{}4 & \texttt{X\_h = t} for \textbf{one} \texttt{h $\neq$ i} & \texttt{X\_i $\neq$ t} \\
generated, rule 1 & \texttt{X\_\{i'\} = t} for \textbf{every} \texttt{i' $\neq$ i} & \texttt{X\_i $\neq$ t} \\
\hline
\end{tabular}\par}
Our premise implies theirs --- a conjunction over all \texttt{i' $\neq$ i} entails the single literal at any witness \texttt{h}. Theirs does not imply ours once there are two other variables. So the published rule fires whenever ours does and, for \texttt{n $\geq$ 3}, in strictly more states: \textbf{ours is strictly weaker}, and the comparison is settled at every arity, not sampled.

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \textbf{\texttt{n = 2}} --- the two rules \textbf{coincide}. The conjunction has exactly one conjunct.
\item \textbf{\texttt{n $\geq$ 3}} --- strictly weaker, and in the sharpest way available: the premise asserts that \texttt{n$-$1 $\geq$ 2} variables all take \texttt{t}, which \textbf{contradicts \texttt{alldifferent} itself}. The rule is sound and can never fire. The published rule fires on the first variable fixed.
\end{itemize}
\textbf{The validator cannot see any of this, and that is the wave's headline.} Rule 1 is \texttt{SOUND and MINIMAL} at \texttt{n,m $\in$ \{2,3,4\}} (my own run, below) and is dead at every \texttt{n $\geq$ 3}. It escapes even the \texttt{VACUOUS} flag, because that flag asks whether \emph{any} store in the enumerated scope satisfies the premises (\texttt{docs/\allowbreak{}VALIDATOR.\allowbreak{}md:227-\allowbreak{}228}) and \texttt{n = 2} is in scope. \textbf{Sound and minimal is a floor, not strength} --- \texttt{catalog/README.md} asserts that sentence, and this is the measurement behind it, against Downing, Feydy and Stuckey \S{}4.

\textbf{\S{}5 and \S{}6: out of reach, and E4 is necessary but not sufficient.} Both quantify their premises over an object that exists only at propagation time --- a Hall set \texttt{H} with endpoints \texttt{a, b} found by a union-find sweep (\S{}5), and the node sets of an SCC of the residual graph of a bipartite matching (\S{}6). C2 classifies both \texttt{per-propagation}. The printer quantifies over declared index sets and has no expression for either. Counting across sums (E4) would supply the \emph{counting} argument; it would not supply a run-time set to quantify over. Same negative result as \texttt{gcc.md} reaches for the flow rule.

\textbf{This sharpens the Scope section above.} That section says \texttt{X\_i = t} is unreachable here and that E4 is what it needs. True of a rule of \emph{this method's kind}; but the published rule that concludes an equality is \S{}6, Example 6.4 (\texttt{[x1 $\neq$ 2] $\rightarrow$ [x1 = 1]}, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}), and it comes from an SCC, not from a sum. E4 is not the route to the published equality rule.

\textbf{\S{}7 is the near miss}, and the only published \texttt{alldifferent} explanation that comes out of a decomposition at all. It needs \textbf{E1} (integer auxiliaries \texttt{c[i]}, \texttt{s[i]}) \textbf{plus E4} (\texttt{c[i] = $\Sigma$\_\allowbreak{}j bool2int(x[j] = i)} reasons across two sums of the same Booleans) --- C2's reading, independently matched by \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:116}. Its reach is stated in the paper: value consistency plus Hall intervals aligned to the ends of \texttt{min(E)..max(E)} (\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}) --- so even reaching \S{}7 would not reach \S{}5.

Per D-0013, ``sound but strictly weaker than Downing's'' is a \textbf{result}, not a failure.

\litsubsub{From \texttt{catalog/\_\allowbreak{}literature/alldifferent.md}, section ``Comparison with this repo's generated entry'' --- that file tags this section \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} throughout: it is the reading of the session that sourced the paper, not a claim the paper makes}
\provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} throughout this section --- this is C2's reading, not a claim about the paper.

\texttt{cata/alldifferent.tex} emits one rule (measured: \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}o '\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}frac' cata/\allowbreak{}alldifferent.\allowbreak{}tex | wc -\allowbreak{}l} $\rightarrow$ 1, run 2026-09-21):

\genrulehead{The \emph{generated} rule, quoted back by the source file. Its verbatim copy is under ``Generated rules'' above.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_\{i'\} = t,~~$\forall$i',~~i' $\neq$ i,~~i' $\in$ $\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$,~~i $\in$ $\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ------------------------------- $\vdash$}
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_i $\neq$ t}
\par}
The published counterpart is rule 1 above, \texttt{[x\_h = v] $\rightarrow$ [x\_i $\neq$ v]}. The two differ in the quantifier on the premise: the paper's premise is a \emph{single} literal for \emph{one} witness \texttt{h}, the generated premise is a conjunction over \emph{all} \texttt{i' $\neq$ i}. The generated rule is therefore \textbf{sound but strictly weaker} than \S{}4's for every \texttt{n > 2}, and coincides with it only at \texttt{n = 2}. That matches \texttt{CLAUDE.md}'s note that this rule ``only ever fires at \texttt{n=2}'', and it is a \textbf{calibration result} in D-0013's sense, not a bug.

The second rule \texttt{CLAUDE.md} says \texttt{alldifferent} should \emph{not} have (concluding \texttt{X\_i = t}) does exist in the literature --- it is the equality deduction of \S{}6, Example 6.4, and it is reached there from an SCC, not from a sum. That is consistent with \texttt{CLAUDE.md}'s trap note that getting it requires \textbf{E4}.

\par\smallskip
\end{calblock}

\begin{pubblock}
\pubhead{Published explanation --- not this project's output}
{\small\sffamily Everything between this bar's ends is \textbf{someone else's published result}, summarised under citation in \texttt{catalog/\_\allowbreak{}literature/cumulative.md} and reproduced here with that file's provenance tags carried across unchanged. Nothing inside was produced by the generator in this repository, and no tag is upgraded: \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} means the formula was transcribed from the fetched document, \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} that it was obtained by a substitution the paper states in prose, \provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY} that only a summary supports it, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} that it could not be obtained at all, and \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} that the sentence is the source file's own reading rather than a claim about the paper. Tags occurring in this file: \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}, \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED}, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY}.\par}
\litsub{Citation}
\textbf{Andreas Schutt, Thibaut Feydy, Peter J. Stuckey, Mark G. Wallace. \emph{Explaining the cumulative propagator.} Constraints 16(3):250-282, 2011. DOI 10.1007/s10601-010-9103-2.}

Read as the authors' \textbf{Constraints manuscript preprint}, \url{https://people.eng.unimelb.edu.au/pstuckey/papers/cumulative.pdf} (fetched 2026-09-21; 710.1 KB). Its first line is ``Constraints manuscript No. (will be inserted by the editor)'', so it is the accepted manuscript, not the typeset article. The preprint \textbf{is} internally paginated (its own running page numbers 1..n), and every page pointer below is a \emph{preprint} page number. \textbf{The journal pagination 250-282 was not cross-checked} --- preprint p.12 is not journal p.261. Repo index: \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:167}, \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:264}.

Notation: the paper writes literals as \texttt{$\llbracket$$\cdot$$\rrbracket$} (double brackets), \texttt{s[i]} for the start-time variable of task \texttt{i}, \texttt{d[i]} its duration, \texttt{r[i]} its resource requirement, \texttt{c} the capacity, \texttt{lb}/\texttt{ub} for current bounds.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{The paper's definition of explanation strength}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}3, preprint p.7:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``Propagation can be explained by different set of clauses. In order to get maximum benefit from the explanation we desire a ''strongest`` explanation as possible. A set of clauses \texttt{C1} is stronger than a set of clauses \texttt{C2} if \texttt{C1} implies \texttt{C2}. In other words, \texttt{C1} restricts the search space at least as much as \texttt{C2}.''
\end{quotation}
Example 4 (same page) shows three explanations of one propagation of which ``The second and third explanation are stronger than the first, but neither of the second or third explanation is stronger than the other.'' \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. \textbf{So the paper's ordering is a partial order and it explicitly does not claim a unique strongest explanation.} This is the single most important sentence in this file for the catalog: the paper's ``stronger'' is \emph{logical implication between explanation clause sets}, which is a different property from the validator's \emph{minimality} (no premise droppable).

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Part A --- consistency check (failure), \S{}6.1, preprint p.12}
\textbf{The overload condition.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.1, preprint p.12. If an overload occurs on a resource of capacity \texttt{c} in the time interval \texttt{[s .. e$-$1]} involving the task set \texttt{$\Omega$}:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$i $\in$ $\Omega$ : ub(s[i]) $\leq$ s~~$\wedge$~~e $\leq$ lb(s[i]) + d[i]}
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\Sigma$\_\{i$\in$$\Omega$\} r[i] > c}
\par}
\litsubsub{A1. Na\"{\i}ve explanation --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.1, preprint p.12}
\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\bigwedge$\_\{i$\in$$\Omega$\} $\llbracket$lb(s[i]) $\leq$ s[i]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[i] $\leq$ ub(s[i])$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ false}
\par}
\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``The na\"{\i}ve explanation simply uses the current domains of all variables involved in the inconsistency, which is always a correct explanation for any constraint.'' \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}
\end{quotation}
\litsubsub{A2. Big-step explanation --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.1, preprint p.12}
\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$i $\in$ $\Omega$ : $\llbracket$e $-$ d[i] $\leq$ s[i]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[i] $\leq$ s$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ false}
\par}
(The display's \texttt{$\forall$i $\in$ $\Omega$} is the paper's own way of writing the conjunction over \texttt{$\Omega$}.) Motivation, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``In some cases some task in \texttt{$\Omega$} might have compulsory parts before or after the overload. These parts are not related to the overload, and give us the possibility to widen the bounds in the explanation.''

\litsubsub{A3. Pointwise explanation --- \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} from a substitution the paper states}
The paper does \textbf{not} display this one. It says, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.1, preprint p.12:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``We can instead explain the overload by concentrating on a single time point \texttt{t} in \texttt{[s .. e$-$1]} rather than examining the whole time interval. ... The explanation has the same pattern as a maximal explanation except we use \texttt{t} for \texttt{s} and \texttt{t + 1} for \texttt{e}. We call these explanations pointwise explanations. The pointwise and big-step explanation coincide iff \texttt{s + 1 = e}.''
\end{quotation}
Applying that substitution to A2 gives

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$i $\in$ $\Omega$ : $\llbracket$t + 1 $-$ d[i] $\leq$ s[i]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[i] $\leq$ t$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ false}
\par}
\textbf{Check against the paper's own Example 8} (preprint p.13, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}): with \texttt{$\Omega$ = \{b,e,f\}}, \texttt{t = 5}, and \texttt{d[b] = 6} (from Example 6, preprint p.9), the paper's minimal explanation is \texttt{$\llbracket$$-\allowbreak{}$1 $\leq$ s\_\allowbreak{}b$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s\_\allowbreak{}b $\leq$ 5$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$1 $\leq$ s\_\allowbreak{}e$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s\_\allowbreak{}e $\leq$ 5$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$$-\allowbreak{}$1 $\leq$ s\_\allowbreak{}f$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s\_\allowbreak{}f $\leq$ 5$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ false}. For \texttt{b}: \texttt{t+1$-$d[b] = 5+1$-$6 = $-$1} $\checkmark$ and \texttt{s[b] $\leq$ 5} $\checkmark$. The substitution reproduces the paper's instance.

\textbf{Terminology caveat.} The quoted sentence says ``the same pattern as a \textbf{maximal} explanation'', but the displayed formula A2 is introduced as the \textbf{big-step} explanation and the word ``maximal'' is not otherwise defined in \S{}6.1. Example 8 then calls A2's instance ``The maximal explanation''. So ``maximal'' = ``big-step'' in this paper. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (both usages transcribed); the identification is \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\litsubsub{The task set \texttt{$\Omega$} need not be minimal --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.1, preprint p.13}
\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``Sometimes a resource overload is detected where the task set \texttt{$\Omega$} of tasks which are compulsory at that time is not minimal with respect to the resource limit, i.e. there exists a proper subset of tasks \texttt{$\Omega$$'$ $\subset$ $\Omega$} with \texttt{$\Sigma$\_\{i$\in$$\Omega$$'$\} r[i] > c}. ... we know the context of the tasks involved and can decide which subset \texttt{$\Omega$$'$} is used in order to explain the inconsistency if there exists a choice. Here as well, it is an open question which subset is the best ... For our experiments the lexicographic least set of tasks is chosen''.
\end{quotation}
\textbf{This is an explicit statement that the shipped explanation is not minimal and that minimality is left open.} Directly relevant to the catalog's minimality column.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Part B --- time-table filtering (bound change), \S{}6.2, preprint pp.14-15}
\textbf{The profile.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.14:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``A profile is a triple \texttt{(A, B, C)} where \texttt{A = [s .. e$-$1]} is a time interval, \texttt{B} the set of all tasks \texttt{i} with \texttt{ub(s[i]) $\leq$ s} and \texttt{lb(s[i]) + d[i] $\geq$ e} (that is a compulsory part in the time interval \texttt{[s .. e$-$1]}), and \texttt{C} the sum of the resource requirements \texttt{r[i]} of all tasks \texttt{i} in \texttt{B}.''
\end{quotation}
\textbf{The propagation.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, same page: when \texttt{lb(s[j])} can be raised to \texttt{LB[j]}, ``there exist a sequence of profiles \texttt{[D1, ..., Dp]} where \texttt{Di = ([si .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{} ei$-\allowbreak{}$1], Bi, Ci)} where \texttt{e0 = lb(s[j])} and \texttt{ep = LB[j]} such that''

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$1 $\leq$ i $\leq$ p : Ci + r[j] > c~~$\wedge$~~si $\leq$ e\_\{i$-$1\} + d[j]}
\par}
``Hence each profile \texttt{Di} pushes the start time of task \texttt{j} to \texttt{ei}.''

\litsubsub{B1. Na\"{\i}ve --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.14}
\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small ( $\llbracket$lb(s[j]) $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\bigwedge$\_\{1$\leq$i$\leq$p, l$\in$Bi\} $\llbracket$lb(s[l]) $\leq$ s[l]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[l] $\leq$ ub(s[l])$\rrbracket$ ) $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$LB[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$}
\par}
\litsubsub{B2. Big-step over the whole sequence --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.14}
\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small ( $\llbracket$s1 + 1 $-$ d[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\bigwedge$\_\{1$\leq$i$\leq$p, l$\in$Bi\} $\llbracket$ei $-$ d[l] $\leq$ s[l]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[l] $\leq$ si$\rrbracket$ ) $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$LB[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$}
\par}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, immediately after: ``Both the above explanations are likely to be very large (they involve all start times appearing in the sequence of profiles) and hence are not likely to be very reusable.''

\litsubsub{B3. Per-profile (``iterative profile'') --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.14}
An explanation for the single profile \texttt{Di = ([si .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{} ei$-\allowbreak{}$1], Bi, Ci)} forcing \texttt{s[j]} from \texttt{e\_\{i$-$1\}} to \texttt{ei}:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small ( $\llbracket$si + 1 $-$ d[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\bigwedge$\_\{l$\in$Bi\} $\llbracket$ei $-$ d[l] $\leq$ s[l]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[l] $\leq$ si$\rrbracket$ ) $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$ei $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$}
\par}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``This corresponds to a big-step explanation of inconsistency over the time interval \texttt{[si .. ei$-$1]}.''

\litsubsub{B4. Pointwise --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.15. \textbf{This is the one the paper uses.}}
\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``Let \texttt{[t1, ..., tm]} be a set of time points such that \texttt{t0 = lb(s[j])}, \texttt{tm + 1 = LB[j]}, \texttt{$\forall$1 $\leq$ j $\leq$ m : t\_\allowbreak{}\{j$-\allowbreak{}$1\} + d[j] $\geq$ tj} and there exists a mapping \texttt{P(tl)} of time points to profiles such that \texttt{$\forall$1 $\leq$ l $\leq$ m : s\_\allowbreak{}\{P(tl)\} $\leq$ tl < e\_\allowbreak{}\{P(tl)\}}. Then we build a pointwise explanation for each time point \texttt{tl}, \texttt{1 $\leq$ l $\leq$ m}''

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small ( $\llbracket$tl + 1 $-$ d[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\bigwedge$\_\{k$\in$Bi\} $\llbracket$tl + 1 $-$ d[l] $\leq$ s[k]$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$s[k] $\leq$ tl$\rrbracket$ ) $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$tl + 1 $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$}
\par}
\textbf{Transcription note.} The index hygiene in this display is the paper's, and it is broken: the conjunction binds \texttt{k $\in$ Bi} (with \texttt{i} unbound at this point --- it should be \texttt{B\_\{P(tl)\}}) yet writes \texttt{d[l]} where \texttt{l} is the \emph{time-point} index, not a task; by the pattern of B3 and by Example 9 it must be \texttt{d[k]}. Transcribed exactly as printed; the two corrections are \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}. The repo will recognise the failure mode --- it is the binder collision of D-0009.

\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, on the choice of points: ``We use these pointwise explanations in our experiments, by starting from \texttt{t0 = lb(s[j])} and for \texttt{j $\in$ [1 .. m]} we choose \texttt{tj} as the greatest time maintains the conditions above. The exception is that if we never entirely skip a profile \texttt{Di} even if this is possible, but instead choose \texttt{ei $-$ 1} as the next time point and continue the process. Our experiments show this is slightly preferable to the skipping a profile entirely.''

\textbf{Worked instance, Example 9, preprint p.15, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}} (\texttt{lb(sf)=0}, \texttt{LB[f]=10}, profiles \texttt{[D1,D2] = [([4.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}7],\{b,e\},4), ([9.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}10],\{c\},4)]}):

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item na\"{\i}ve: \texttt{$\llbracket$2 $\leq$ sb$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$sb $\leq$ 3$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$2 $\leq$ se$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$se $\leq$ 4$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$8 $\leq$ sc$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$sf $\leq$ 9$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$10 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$}
\item iterative profile: \texttt{$\llbracket$1 $\leq$ sb$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$sb $\leq$ 4$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$2 $\leq$ se$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$se $\leq$ 4$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$7 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$} and \texttt{$\llbracket$4 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$8 $\leq$ sc$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$sc $\leq$ 9$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$10 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$}
\item iterative pointwise (points 5 and 9): \texttt{$\llbracket$sb $\leq$ 5$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$1 $\leq$ se$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$se $\leq$ 5$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$6 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$} and \texttt{$\llbracket$4 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$8 $\leq$ sc$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\llbracket$sc $\leq$ 9$\rrbracket$ $\rightarrow$ $\llbracket$10 $\leq$ sf$\rrbracket$}
\end{itemize}
and, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``Note that this explanation is analogous to the explanation devised by the decomposition in Example 6, and stronger than the iterative profile explanation.''

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Translation to this repo's notation}
The \textbf{body} of B4 translates; the \textbf{quantification} does not.

\rulekind{Published rule, in the source file's attempted translation --- which that file marks \emph{not faithful}. Printed in the file's own layout, not as a rule of this catalog; the reasons follow.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small S\_j $\geq$ t+1$-$d\_j,~~~S\_k $\geq$ t+1$-$d\_k,~~~S\_k $\leq$ t,~~~$\forall$k $\in$ B}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ------------------------------------------------- $\vdash$~~~~~~~~(BC)}
\litline{\ttfamily\small S\_j $\geq$ t+1}
\par}
Faithful in shape. \textbf{Not faithful as a schema}, for three independent reasons:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \texttt{B} is the set of tasks with a compulsory part at \texttt{t}. Membership is a \emph{run-time} predicate on current bounds (\texttt{ub(s[k]) $\leq$ t < lb(s[k]) + d[k]}), not an index set the printer can name. The repo's printer has \texttt{$\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$}, \texttt{$\llbracket$1,m$\rrbracket$} and an undefined \texttt{D\_k} beyond that (\texttt{CLAUDE.md}, ``Traps'').
\item The premises carry \textbf{arithmetic on thresholds}, \texttt{t + 1 $-$ d[k]}. The repo's event type is \texttt{(sign, variable, index list, AC|BC)} with no term arithmetic; \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:167} calls this \textbf{E2}.
\item \texttt{d[i]} and \texttt{r[i]} are \emph{variables} in the general \texttt{cumulative}, and the capacity argument \texttt{$\Sigma$\_\{i$\in$B\} r[i] + r[j] > c} is a sum comparison across tasks --- \textbf{E4} by the same line.
\end{enumerate}
\provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} for all three; \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:167} reaches the same conclusion independently (``Needs \textbf{E2} ... and \textbf{E4} ... \emph{The other key test case.}'').

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Answers to the four catalog questions}
\textbf{What event is explained?} Both: failure (\S{}6.1, the overload check) and a \textbf{bound change} (\S{}6.2, \texttt{$\llbracket$LB[j] $\leq$ s[j]$\rrbracket$}, i.e. a lower-bound increase; the paper says ``the case of decreasing upper bounds in analogous and omitted'', \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, preprint p.14). \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\textbf{Premises' vocabulary?} \textbf{User variables only} --- start times \texttt{s[i]} with shifted thresholds. No auxiliaries appear in the \emph{global} propagator's explanations. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

Contrast with the paper's own \textbf{TimeD decomposition} (\S{}5.1, preprint p.9), which \emph{does} introduce auxiliaries, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$t $\in$ [0 .. tmax$-$1], $\forall$i $\in$ [1 .. n] : B\_it $\leftrightarrow$ $\llbracket$s[i] $\leq$ t$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\neg$$\llbracket$s[i] $\leq$ t $-$ d[i]$\rrbracket$}
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\forall$t $\in$ [0 .. tmax$-$1] : $\Sigma$\_\{i$\in$[1..n]\} r[i] $\cdot$ B\_it $\leq$ c}
\par}
This is a \texttt{rule1}-style reified equivalence over a conjunction, feeding a Boolean-sum-\texttt{$\leq$} (\texttt{rule5}) --- structurally \emph{exactly} the shape this repo's generator consumes, except that the sum is weighted by \texttt{r[i]} and the second conjunct is a negated literal at a shifted threshold. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}. The paper's verdict on the two, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.15: ``The global cumulative using time-table filtering and the TimeD decomposition have the same propagation strength. The advantages of the global approach is that we can control the times points we propagate on ... The possible advantage of the decomposition is that it learns smaller nogoods related to the decomposed variables, but since \texttt{B\_it} simply represents a fixed conjunction of bounds in practice the nogoods learned by the TimeD decomposition have no advantage.''

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} \textbf{No.} The paper gives a \emph{ladder} of explanations ordered by its own implication-strength relation (na\"{\i}ve < big-step < pointwise) and says pointwise is the strongest it uses --- but it explicitly leaves two minimality questions open (\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, preprint pp.13): which time point \texttt{t} to pick (``does it matter which time point is picked, if so, which is the best one?'') and which subset \texttt{$\Omega$$'$} of an over-large task set to use (``it is an open question which subset is the best''). Example 8 calls the pointwise instance ``A minimal explanation'' (\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, preprint p.13), but that is the label on one instance, not a theorem; no minimality is proved anywhere in the paper.

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Per-propagation.} \texttt{B} (the compulsory-part set), the profile sequence \texttt{[D1..Dp]} and the chosen time points \texttt{[t1..tm]} all come from scanning the resource time-table profile built at propagation time. The \emph{form} of B3/B4 is a fixed template, so it is closer to a schema than \texttt{alldifferent}'s SCC explanation is --- but the index set the template quantifies over is computed, and a decomposition-derived generator would have to name it. The paper's own TimeD decomposition dodges this by quantifying over \emph{all} times \texttt{t} and letting \texttt{B\_it} be false where the task is not compulsory; that \textbf{is} a schema, and it is the form this repo could in principle reach. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{What was not sourced \ \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}}
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item The typeset Constraints 16(3):250-282 article itself (paywalled at Springer; not fetched). Everything above is from the authors' accepted manuscript, which may differ in pagination and in copy-editing. \provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY} \textbf{only} in that sense --- the content is quoted from a document by the same authors bearing the same title, not from a third-party summary.
\item The upper-bound (decreasing) form of B1-B4. The paper says it is ``analogous and omitted''. \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} (the paper does not give it).
\item The \textbf{task decomposition} (TaskD, \S{}5.2) explanations were not transcribed; only TimeD, which is the one structurally comparable to this repo. Fetch budget, not availability --- the section begins at preprint p.10 for anyone who needs it.
\end{itemize}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{How the source file was produced}
{\small
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \texttt{WebFetch https:/\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}people.\allowbreak{}eng.\allowbreak{}unimelb.\allowbreak{}edu.\allowbreak{}au/\allowbreak{}pstuckey/\allowbreak{}papers/\allowbreak{}cumulative.\allowbreak{}pdf} (2026-09-21). The URL was a guess from the naming pattern of the same group's other preprints; it resolved to the Constraints accepted manuscript. The fetch tool's summariser could not read the PDF; it saved the binary, which was then run through \texttt{pdftotext -raw} locally. \textbf{Every quote above is transcribed from that local text.}
\item Page pointers: \texttt{pdftotext -\allowbreak{}raw -\allowbreak{}f <p> -\allowbreak{}l <p> cumu.\allowbreak{}pdf -\allowbreak{} | grep -\allowbreak{}n '<phrase>'}, one page at a time, for every phrase cited. Several first-draft page numbers in this file were wrong by one or two and were corrected this way before committing.
\item The A3 pointwise consistency rule is \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED}, and the derivation was \textbf{checked} against the paper's Example 8 arithmetic (\texttt{t=5}, \texttt{d[b]=6} from Example 6, preprint p.9 $\rightarrow$ \texttt{$-$1 $\leq$ s\_b}), not just asserted.
\item The minimality claim is a \textbf{measurement}: \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n -\allowbreak{}i 'minimal' <text>} over the whole preprint returns 5 hits, quoted or accounted for above; none of them is a theorem or a proof obligation, two of them are the open questions quoted in \S{}6.1.
\item \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md} line numbers from \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n -\allowbreak{}i 'Schutt' CHRISTMAS\_\allowbreak{}LIST.\allowbreak{}md}.
\end{itemize}
\par}
\end{pubblock}

\begin{calblock}
\calhead{Calibration --- the generated rule against the published one}
{\small\sffamily Verdict, from this entry's own header table: \textbf{out of reach} --- but, uniquely among the three entries, out of reach by \emph{gaps} (G11, G15), not by structure.\par}
\litsubsub{From \texttt{catalog/cumulative.md}, section ``Calibration (W3-T5, D-0013)''}
\textbf{Verdict: out of reach} --- and this is the one entry of the three where that verdict is about \textbf{gaps this project has numbered}, not about structure. No implication comparison is stated, for two independent reasons: the shipped rules explain \texttt{disjunctive}, not \texttt{cumulative}, and they have \textbf{no verdict at all} (\texttt{not validatable}), so there is nothing established to compare.

\textbf{What makes it the interesting case: the paper's own decomposition is this generator's exact shape.} Schutt et al. \S{}5.1's TimeD is

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small B\_it~~$\leftrightarrow$~~$\llbracket$s\_i $\leq$ t$\rrbracket$ $\wedge$ $\neg$$\llbracket$s\_i $\leq$ t $-$ d\_i$\rrbracket$~~~~~~~~~~~~(a rule1 reified equivalence over a rule3 $\wedge$)}
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\Sigma$\_i r\_i $\cdot$ B\_it~~$\leq$~~c~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(a rule5 Boolean sum $\leq$)}
\par}
and the shipped \texttt{cumul} chain (\texttt{explenation generator.\allowbreak{}ml:810-\allowbreak{}812}) is \texttt{rule1} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{rule3} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{rule5} in exactly that order, over exactly that pair of auxiliaries: a reified bound literal, a conjunction of a duration-shifted literal with the negation of an unshifted one, and a Boolean sum \texttt{$\leq$}. \textbf{The shipped decomposition is TimeD at \texttt{r\_i = 1}, \texttt{c = 1}} --- TimeD specialised to a unary resource, with the bound implicit.

\emph{This identification is C3's reading}, from C2's \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} display of TimeD set beside the generator source. C2 states the structural match as \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} (``structurally \emph{exactly} the shape this repo's generator consumes''); nothing here upgrades that tag. Two caveats on it: the literal polarity differs (our \texttt{B1} is \texttt{X\_i $\geq$ t}, TimeD's is \texttt{$\llbracket$s\_i $\leq$ t$\rrbracket$}, and the negated conjunct swaps with it), and the \emph{direction} of the duration shift cannot be read off the source at all, because the index functions are opaque closures (D-0006's first item).

\textbf{And the paper states what that shape reaches}, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2: ``The global cumulative using time-table filtering and the TimeD decomposition have the same propagation strength.'' That sentence is about TimeD in general; the paper does not separately state the unary case, and this entry does not claim it does.

\textbf{So the verdict is a gap between the shape and the entry, and the gap is two items long.} What separates the shipped artifact from TimeD is exactly:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \textbf{G11} --- the sum is unweighted. TimeD's is \texttt{$\Sigma$\_i r\_i $\cdot$ B\_it}; there is no encoding for \texttt{r\_i $\cdot$ B\_it} (E8 under D-0011).
\item \textbf{G15} --- the threshold shift \texttt{t $-$ d\_i} is arithmetic on a variable's \emph{value}, and the artifact carries \texttt{d\_i} as an uninterpreted \texttt{ind\_const} symbol. That is the same defect the validator reports mechanically as \texttt{UNPARSED: index equation offset: t'=t-\allowbreak{}d\_\allowbreak{}\{i\}}, so it blocks the verdict and the calibration for one reason.
\end{itemize}
Close those two and the generator is on TimeD, whose propagation strength the paper equates to the global time-table propagator's. \textbf{That is the strongest positive statement available anywhere in this wave, and it is conditional}: nothing is measured, no rule here has a verdict, and the claim is about a shape the generator cannot yet emit.

\textbf{Two routes, different blockers --- do not merge them.} C1's prior (recorded here before C2's sourcing) expected ``weaker: names \texttt{n} tasks where Schutt names a subset''. That is right about the \textbf{global} \S{}6.2 explanations, whose premises are quantified over a compulsory-part set \texttt{B} and a chosen sequence of time points --- run-time objects, out of reach for the same structural reason as \texttt{gcc.md}'s cut, and needing \textbf{E4} on top. It does \textbf{not} transfer to the TimeD route: TimeD quantifies over \emph{all} times \texttt{t} and lets \texttt{B\_it} be false where the task is not compulsory, so no run-time subset is ever named (C2, \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}). One paper, two targets: the global rule is structurally out of reach, the decomposition is two gaps away.

\textbf{One correction to C1's prior.} It proposed comparing ``against the unary special case of the same papers''. The sourced paper gives no unary special case of its explanations; the comparable object is TimeD, and the unary specialisation is this repo's, not Schutt et al.'s.

\end{calblock}
\subsubsection*{\texttt{global\_\allowbreak{}cardinality}}
\label{entry:gcc}

\entryfield{Validator}{4 \texttt{SOUND and MINIMAL}, 0 flagged --- \textbf{the only fully validated entry in the repo}}
\entryfield{Calibration}{\textbf{out of reach} --- the published rule is a run-time flow cut / SCC; these 4 rules answer a different question}
\entryfield{Last measured}{2026-09-21, \texttt{make validate} (re-run by C3, same verdicts) and \texttt{python3 tools/\allowbreak{}mzn\_\allowbreak{}coverage.\allowbreak{}py -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}rank -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}json}}
\entryfield{Blocking gap}{none stated in the Status row}

\textbf{Generated rules} --- this project's output, copied verbatim from \texttt{cata/gcc.tex} (4, counted as occurrences of \texttt{\textbackslash{}frac}).\par

{\small\textbf{Generated rule 1 of 4} --- \texttt{cata/gcc.tex}, this project's generator. \textsc{verdict} SOUND and MINIMAL\par}
\rulebox{\frac{X_{i'} \neq t,~\forall i',~i' \neq i,~i' \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket~~~~O_{t} \geq p,~p \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket}{X_{i}=t}}

{\small\textbf{Generated rule 2 of 4} --- \texttt{cata/gcc.tex}, this project's generator. \textsc{verdict} SOUND and MINIMAL\par}
\rulebox{\frac{X_{i'}=t,~\forall i',~i' \neq i,~i' \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket~~~~O_{t}<p,~p \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket}{X_{i} \neq t}}

{\small\textbf{Generated rule 3 of 4} --- \texttt{cata/gcc.tex}, this project's generator. \textsc{verdict} SOUND and MINIMAL\par}
\rulebox{\frac{X_{i}=t,~\forall i,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket,~\forall i,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket}{O_{t} \geq p}}

{\small\textbf{Generated rule 4 of 4} --- \texttt{cata/gcc.tex}, this project's generator. \textsc{verdict} SOUND and MINIMAL\par}
\rulebox{\frac{X_{i} \neq t,~\forall i,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket,~\forall i,~i \in \llbracket1,n\rrbracket}{O_{t}<p}}
