\begin{pubblock}
\pubhead{Published explanation --- not this project's output}
{\small\sffamily Everything between this bar's ends is \textbf{someone else's published result}, summarised under citation in \texttt{catalog/\_\allowbreak{}literature/gcc.md} and reproduced here with that file's provenance tags carried across unchanged. Nothing inside was produced by the generator in this repository, and no tag is upgraded: \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} means the formula was transcribed from the fetched document, \provtag{tagderived}{DERIVED} that it was obtained by a substitution the paper states in prose, \provtag{tagsecondary}{SECONDARY} that only a summary supports it, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} that it could not be obtained at all, and \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} that the sentence is the source file's own reading rather than a claim about the paper. Tags occurring in this file: \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}, \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.\par}
\litsub{Citation}
\textbf{Nicholas Downing, Thibaut Feydy, Peter J. Stuckey. \emph{Explaining flow-based propagation.} Proc. CPAIOR 2012, LNCS 7298:146-162, Springer. DOI 10.1007/978-3-642-29828-8\_10.}

Read as the authors' preprint, \url{https://people.eng.unimelb.edu.au/pstuckey/papers/explaining_flow.pdf} (fetched 2026-09-21; 2.4 MB, 19 pages). The preprint carries \textbf{no printed page numbers}, so every page pointer below is a \emph{preprint PDF page index}. LNCS pagination 146-162 not cross-checked. Repo index: \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:127}, \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:267}.

\textbf{Headline, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, abstract (preprint p.1):} the paper presents ``two new generic flow-based propagators'' with ``the addition of explanation capability'', and says these ``can efficiently replace specialized versions, in particular for gcc and sequence''.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{The structural fact the catalog most needs}
\textbf{There is no \texttt{gcc}-specific explanation rule in this paper.} \texttt{gcc} is handled by \emph{encoding it as a flow network} (R\'egin's encoding) and then explaining the \textbf{generic} network-flow propagator. Everything below is therefore stated over flow variables \texttt{f\_uv}, with \texttt{gcc} appearing only through the identification of arc flows with domain literals. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} --- see the encoding sentence next.

\textbf{The encoding.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}3 Example 1, preprint p.3:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``This illustrates R\'egin's [18] encoding of the constraint \texttt{gcc([x, y], [1.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}2, 0.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}1])}, with \texttt{x, y = 1} (day) or \texttt{2} (night) being the shift worked by Xavier (\texttt{x}) and Yasmin (\texttt{y}). Using the coercion function \texttt{bool2int}, the 'working arc' flows are expressed directly as domain literals which are intrinsic in a Lazy Clause Generation solver, e.g. \texttt{f\_xd = bool2int($\llbracket$x = 1$\rrbracket$)}, where \texttt{bool2int(false) = 0} and \texttt{bool2int(true) = 1}.''
\end{quotation}
So: \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}\{i,v\} = bool2int([x\_\allowbreak{}i = v])} for the variable-value arcs, and the \texttt{f\_\{v,t\}} arcs carry the cardinality counts \texttt{c\_v}, bounded by the \texttt{gcc} cover bounds.

\textbf{Flow conservation for a cut.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}4.1, preprint p.6, \textbf{equation (2)}. Summing the flow-conservation equations (1) over the nodes \texttt{n $\in$ C} gives

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\Sigma$\_\{(u,v) leaves C\} f\_uv~~$-$~~$\Sigma$\_\{(u,v) enters C\} f\_uv~~=~~$\Sigma$\_\{n$\in$C\} s\_n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(2)}
\par}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 1 --- failure}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}4.1 ``Explaining failure'', preprint pp.4-6:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``Suppose there is no feasible solution. Let \texttt{C}, the 'cut', be the set of nodes searched for an augmenting path. It contains node(s) in excess but none in deficit. Then according to the current flow bounds, more flow enters \texttt{C} than can leave it, taking into account the arcs crossing \texttt{C} and the net supply/demand of \texttt{C}.''
\end{quotation}
and then, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, preprint p.6:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``Given \texttt{C} that proves infeasibility, we explain equation (2) as a linear constraint, using a standard linear explanation for \texttt{LHS $\leq$ RHS} [16]. Even if outflows are at minimum for outgoing arcs and inflows are at maximum for incoming arcs, minimizing the net flow leaving the cut, the net flow is still greater than the net supply/demand of the cut. \textbf{The explanation of failure is the conjunction of literals \texttt{$\llbracket$f\_uv $\geq$ l\_uv$\rrbracket$} for outflows and \texttt{$\llbracket$f\_uv $\leq$ u\_uv$\rrbracket$} for inflows, using current \texttt{l}, \texttt{u}.} Similar explanations were proposed by Rochart [20]. For the special case of gcc they reduce to those proposed by Katsirelos [12]. We can improve the base explanation by using lifting methods [1, 5, 16] to create a stronger explanation.''
\end{quotation}
(Boldface added here; the emphasis is not the paper's.)

\textbf{As a rule:}

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\llbracket$f\_uv $\geq$ l\_uv$\rrbracket$~~for each arc (u,v) leaving C}
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\llbracket$f\_uv $\leq$ u\_uv$\rrbracket$~~for each arc (u,v) entering C}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ----------------------------------------------- $\vdash$}
\litline{\ttfamily\small false}
\par}
\textbf{Worked instance}, \S{}4.1 Example 3, preprint p.6, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}. With \texttt{C = \{x, n, y\}}: ``Cut-conservation (2) requires \texttt{bool2int($\llbracket$x = 1$\rrbracket$) + bool2int($\llbracket$y = 1$\rrbracket$) + f\_\allowbreak{}nt = 2}, unachievable since both literals are false and \texttt{f\_nt $\leq$ 1}. Hence the network flow propagator fails with nogood''

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x $\neq$ 1] $\wedge$ [y $\neq$ 1] $\wedge$ [f\_nt $\leq$ 1] $\rightarrow$ false}
\par}
Note the nogood mixes \textbf{user-variable literals} (\texttt{[x $\neq$ 1]}) with \textbf{flow-variable literals} (\texttt{[f\_nt $\leq$ 1]}) --- the latter is a cardinality-count literal in \texttt{gcc} terms.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Rule 2 --- pruning}
\textbf{Paper's form.} \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}4.2 ``Explaining pruning'', preprint pp.6-7:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``R\'egin describes a method based on Strongly Connected Components (SCCs) for gcc constraints [18], which we generalize to any flow network to find all arcs fixed at a bound, that is \texttt{f\_uv = l\_uv} (resp. \texttt{u\_uv}) which cannot increase (resp. decrease). For Boolean flow variables, bound-tightening implies fixing at a bound and vice versa, giving bounds-consistency on Boolean-valued arcs.''
\end{quotation}
\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``An arc \texttt{u $\rightarrow$ v} with \texttt{u, v} in different SCCs can never be augmented since by definition \texttt{u} is not reachable again from \texttt{v}.''
\end{quotation}
\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
``\textbf{The explanation for pruning is the same as for failure, except that an SCC is used as the cut-set \texttt{C} instead of an infeasible set.} Once again we treat equation (2) as a linear '$\leq$' constraint. This relies on the SCC acting as a 'trap' for incoming flow, to prune an incoming flow the bounds on outgoing flows must be tight.''
\end{quotation}
\textbf{As a rule:}

\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\llbracket$f\_uv $\geq$ l\_uv$\rrbracket$~~for each arc (u,v) leaving the SCC C}
\litline{\ttfamily\small $\llbracket$f\_uv $\leq$ u\_uv$\rrbracket$~~for each arc (u,v) entering C, other than the one being pruned}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- $\vdash$}
\litline{\ttfamily\small the pruned bound on the arc being pruned}
\par}
\textbf{Worked instance --- and this is the one the catalog should quote}, \S{}4.2 Example 4, preprint p.7, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}:

\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
"Consider \texttt{alldifferent(x1, x2, x3)}, expressed as the usual gcc network of \texttt{gcc([x1, x2, x3], [c1, c2, c3, c4])} where \texttt{ci $\in$ 0..1}. If \texttt{x1 $\in$ \{1,2\}}, \texttt{x2 $\in$ \{2,3\}}, \texttt{x3 $\in$ \{2,3,4\}}, then a solution is \texttt{x1 = 1, x2 = 2, x3 = 3} ... Due to the cycle \texttt{t $\rightarrow$ 1 $\rightarrow$ x1 $\rightarrow$ 2 $\rightarrow$ x2 $\rightarrow$ 3 $\rightarrow$ x3 $\rightarrow$ 4 $\rightarrow$ t} every node is reachable from each other, the entire graph is a single SCC, and no pruning is possible. Now suppose \texttt{x3 $\neq$ 4} ... Then the arc \texttt{x1 $\rightarrow$ 2} may be pruned due to cut-conservation (2) for SCC \#1: \texttt{bool2int($\llbracket$x3 = 4$\rrbracket$) + c2 + c3 $-\allowbreak{}$ bool2int($\llbracket$x1 = 2$\rrbracket$) = 2} and hence \texttt{bool2int($\llbracket$x1 = 2$\rrbracket$) = 0} since \texttt{$\llbracket$x3 = 4$\rrbracket$ = false}, \texttt{c2 $\leq$ 1}, and \texttt{c3 $\leq$ 1}. The explanation is
\end{quotation}
\rulekind{Published rule, in the paper's own form. Not translated: see the surrounding text for why a translation would misrepresent it.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small [x3 $\neq$ 4] $\wedge$ [c2 $\leq$ 1] $\wedge$ [c3 $\leq$ 1] $\rightarrow$ [x1 $\neq$ 2]}
\par}
\begin{quotation}\small\itshape
or after removing redundant bounds \texttt{[x3 $\neq$ 4] $\rightarrow$ [x1 $\neq$ 2]}. Having pruned all arcs leaving SCC \#2, that SCC is closed, allowing the arc \texttt{x1 $\rightarrow$ 1} to be fixed to true using \texttt{[x1 $\neq$ 2]} as justification and so on."
\end{quotation}
This makes the vocabulary question concrete: the premises are \textbf{user-variable literals} (\texttt{[x3 $\neq$ 4]}) \textbf{plus the cardinality variables of the \texttt{gcc} signature} (\texttt{[c2 $\leq$ 1]}, \texttt{[c3 $\leq$ 1]}). The \texttt{c\_j} are arguments of \texttt{gcc}, not encoding auxiliaries --- but for the \texttt{alldifferent} instance they are auxiliaries introduced by the reduction to \texttt{gcc}. \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON}.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Translation to this repo's notation}
Rule 2 translates \textbf{only as an instance}, never as a schema:

\rulekind{Published rule, in the source file's attempted translation --- which that file marks \emph{not faithful}. Printed in the file's own layout, not as a rule of this catalog; the reasons follow.}
{\raggedright
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_3 $\neq$ 4,~~C\_2 $\leq$ 1,~~C\_3 $\leq$ 1}
\litline{\ttfamily\small ------------------------------- $\vdash$~~~~~~~~(AC)}
\litline{\ttfamily\small X\_1 $\neq$ 2}
\par}
The general rule does \textbf{not} translate. Its quantifier is ``over the arcs crossing an SCC of the residual graph of the current flow'', and:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item There is no index-set expression for ``the arcs crossing SCC \texttt{C}''. The repo's printer has \texttt{$\llbracket$1,n$\rrbracket$}, \texttt{$\llbracket$1,m$\rrbracket$} and an undefined \texttt{D\_k} beyond (\texttt{CLAUDE.md}, ``Traps''); \texttt{C} is not a subscript range, it is the output of Tarjan's algorithm on a graph that exists only at propagation time.
\item The premise \emph{polarity} depends on arc direction relative to the cut (\texttt{$\geq$ l} for outflows, \texttt{$\leq$ u} for inflows). Direction is a property of the run-time residual graph, which flips arcs as flows hit their bounds (\S{}3.1, preprint p.3). A schema over indices has no such notion.
\item The rule is derived by \textbf{treating equation (2) as a linear constraint and applying a linear propagator's explanation to it} --- an explanation \emph{of an explanation}. The repo's engine explains a fixed decomposition, not a linear constraint synthesised per propagation.
\end{enumerate}
\provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} for all three.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{Answers to the four catalog questions}
\textbf{What event is explained?} Both a failure (\S{}4.1) and a bound change / value removal (\S{}4.2, which for Boolean arcs is the same thing --- \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}: ``For Boolean flow variables, bound-tightening implies fixing at a bound and vice versa''). The min-cost extension (\S{}6) also explains \textbf{fathoming} against an objective bound, which has no analogue in this repo at all. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}6.2, preprint p.9.

\textbf{Premises' vocabulary?} \textbf{Flow variables \texttt{f\_uv}.} These are \emph{partly} user variables --- the paper identifies \texttt{f\_\{i,v\}} with the LCG literal \texttt{[x\_i = v]}, so those premises are genuinely over \texttt{x} --- and \emph{partly} the cardinality counts \texttt{c\_j}, which for \texttt{gcc} are signature arguments and for \texttt{alldifferent} are introduced by the reduction. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} (Example 1 for the identification, Example 4 for both kinds appearing in one nogood).

\textbf{Minimal / strongest?} \textbf{No, and the paper says so directly.} The explanation it gives is called ``the base explanation'', and, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}4.1, preprint p.6: ``We can improve the base explanation by using lifting methods [1, 5, 16] to create a stronger explanation.'' Example 4 strips ``redundant bounds'' by hand (\provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}) to get from a three-literal nogood to a one-literal one --- which is exactly the premise-droppability the repo's validator measures, and the paper leaves it as an optional post-pass, not a property of the rule. The conclusions reinforce that the raw explanations are large, \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}, \S{}8 (Experiments), preprint p.13: ``explanations from flow networks can be large (usually hundreds of literals for nurse rostering, more for car sequencing)''.

\textbf{Schema or per-propagation?} \textbf{Per-propagation, unambiguously.} The cut \texttt{C} is ``the set of nodes searched for an augmenting path'' (Ford-Fulkerson) or a strongly connected component of the residual graph (Tarjan). Both are outputs of graph algorithms run at propagation time on a structure the decomposition does not contain. \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED}.

\par\smallskip
\litsub{What was not sourced \ \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED}}
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item The typeset LNCS 7298:146-162 chapter (paywalled at Springer; not fetched). Content above is from the authors' preprint, same title and authors.
\item Katsirelos (2008) and Rochart (2005), which the paper says its explanations reduce to / resemble for the \texttt{gcc} special case. \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} --- not fetched (budget). If the catalog wants the \texttt{gcc}-specific ancestor of this rule, Katsirelos's thesis is the place, and \texttt{alldifferent.md} records that Downing et al.'s ACSC paper also names Katsirelos as the source of its SCC explanation.
\item The soft / min-cost flow explanations (\S{}5, \S{}6) beyond noting that fathoming is explained. \provtag{tagnotsourced}{NOT SOURCED} by choice --- no analogue in this repo.
\end{itemize}
\par\smallskip
\litsub{How the source file was produced}
{\small
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \texttt{WebSearch} for the preprint URL, then \texttt{WebFetch https:/\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}people.\allowbreak{}eng.\allowbreak{}unimelb.\allowbreak{}edu.\allowbreak{}au/\allowbreak{}pstuckey/\allowbreak{}papers/\allowbreak{}explaining\_\allowbreak{}flow.\allowbreak{}pdf} (2026-09-21). The fetch tool's summariser could not read the PDF; it saved the binary, which was then run through \texttt{pdftotext -raw} locally. \textbf{Every quote above is transcribed from that local text.}
\item Page pointers: \texttt{pdftotext -\allowbreak{}raw -\allowbreak{}f <p> -\allowbreak{}l <p> flow.\allowbreak{}pdf -\allowbreak{} | grep -\allowbreak{}n '<phrase>'}, one page at a time, for every phrase cited. The preprint has 19 pages (\texttt{pdfinfo}). Section headings enumerated with \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n '\textasciicircum{}[0-\allowbreak{}9] [A-\allowbreak{}Z]\textbackslash{}|\textasciicircum{}[0-\allowbreak{}9]\textbackslash{}.\allowbreak{}[0-\allowbreak{}9] '}: \S{}1-\S{}9 with \S{}4.1, \S{}4.2, \S{}5.1, \S{}6.1, \S{}6.2.
\item The minimality claim is a \textbf{measurement}: \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n -\allowbreak{}i 'minimal' <text>} over the whole preprint returns \textbf{1} hit, and it is about the magnitude of an arc value in the dual network simplex (\S{}5.1), not about explanations. The paper's own words for its explanations are ``the base explanation'' and ``can be large''.
\item \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md} line numbers from \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}n -\allowbreak{}i 'Downing' CHRISTMAS\_\allowbreak{}LIST.\allowbreak{}md}.
\end{itemize}
\par}
\end{pubblock}

\begin{calblock}
\calhead{Calibration --- the generated rule against the published one}
{\small\sffamily Verdict, from this entry's own header table: \textbf{out of reach} --- the published rule is a run-time flow cut / SCC; these 4 rules answer a different question.\par}
\litsubsub{From \texttt{catalog/gcc.md}, section ``Calibration (W3-T5, D-0013)''}
\textbf{Verdict: out of reach.} Not \texttt{weaker}, not \texttt{incomparable}: the two rules cannot be placed in an implication order at all, because the published premise set is not expressible in this method's vocabulary of index sets. ``Out of reach'' is a first-class verdict (D-0013), and this entry is the clean instance of it.

\textbf{Why no implication comparison can even be stated.} Three obstacles, all from C2's reading of \S{}4.1-\S{}4.2, and each independently fatal:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item \textbf{No index-set expression for the premise's range.} The premises are indexed by ``the arcs crossing the cut \texttt{C}'', where \texttt{C} is the set of nodes searched for an augmenting path, or a strongly connected component of the \emph{residual} graph. The printer quantifies over \texttt{[1,n]}, \texttt{[1,m]} and an undefined \texttt{D\_k} beyond (\texttt{CLAUDE.md}, ``Traps''); \texttt{C} is the output of a graph algorithm run at propagation time.
\item \textbf{Premise polarity depends on arc direction relative to the cut} (\texttt{$\geq$ l} outflow, \texttt{$\leq$ u} inflow), and direction is a property of the residual graph, which flips arcs as flows reach their bounds. A schema over indices has no such notion.
\item \textbf{The rule is an explanation of an explanation} --- equation (2), cut-conservation, is synthesised per propagation and then explained as a linear constraint. This engine explains a fixed decomposition.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{It is not a vocabulary gap, and that distinction is worth keeping.} This entry \emph{does} carry occurrence-count atoms (\texttt{O\_t $\geq$ p}, \texttt{O\_t < p}), and the published worked instance's premises are exactly of that kind --- \texttt{[x3 $\neq$ 4] $\wedge$ [c2 $\leq$ 1] $\wedge$ [c3 $\leq$ 1]} mixes user-variable literals with cardinality-bound literals. What is out of reach is the \textbf{quantification}, not the literals. (This paragraph is C3's reading, built on C2's \provtag{tagquoted}{QUOTED} Example 4; it is not a claim about the paper.)

\textbf{The four rules answer a different question, and scoring them against the flow rule would hide that.} They channel between \texttt{X} literals and bounds on one occurrence variable, within a single sum, for one value \texttt{t} at a time. The published rule reasons \emph{across} values, through the flow network --- which is why it prunes where these cannot. \texttt{4 SOUND and MINIMAL at n,m $\in$ \{2,3,4\}} (my own run, below) is a complete answer to the first question and says nothing about the second. It is also the floor, not strength: rules 1 and 2 still name every other variable.

\textbf{E4 is necessary but not sufficient}, sharpening \texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:127}, which prices the flow explanation at E4. Counting across sums would give access to the cardinality literals; it would not give a cut of a residual graph. C2 puts it as a negative result rather than a gap to close, and this entry adopts that.

\textbf{Two priors recorded by C1, now settled.} C1 expected \texttt{weaker}, with ``incomparable --- different constraint'' as a second axis. Sourcing resolves both to \texttt{out of reach}: the constraint mismatch is real (the paper's network carries cardinality \emph{bounds}, this decomposition has none) but it is not the binding reason --- the binding reason is structural, and it would still hold if the bounds were added.

\litsubsub{From \texttt{catalog/\_\allowbreak{}literature/gcc.md}, section ``Bearing on this repo'' --- that file tags this section \provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} throughout: it is the reading of the session that sourced the paper, not a claim the paper makes}
\provtag{tagcomparison}{COMPARISON} throughout.

\texttt{CHRISTMAS\_LIST.md:127} routes \texttt{global\_cardinality} through \textbf{E3/E9} for the MiniZinc decomposition (\texttt{forall(i)(count(xs,cover[i],count[i])) /\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{} length(xs) >= sum(count)}, whose trailing sum is over \emph{integer} variables) and notes separately that ``The flow explanation needs \textbf{E4}''. This file supports the second half of that and sharpens it: \textbf{E4 is necessary but not sufficient.} Counting across sums would give access to the cardinality literals \texttt{[c\_j $\leq$ 1]}; it would not give access to a cut of a residual graph. The published \texttt{gcc} explanation is \textbf{structurally out of reach} for any method that derives a rule schema from a static decomposition, because its premise set is indexed by a run-time graph object with no static counterpart.

That is a negative result worth recording rather than a gap to close.

\par\smallskip
\end{calblock}

\textbf{Generated rules} --- this project's output, copied verbatim from \texttt{cata/alldifferent\_except.tex} (1, counted as occurrences of \texttt{\textbackslash{}frac}).\par

{\small\textbf{Generated rule 1 of 1} --- \texttt{cata/alldifferent\_except.tex}, this project's generator. \textsc{no verdict} --- this entry does not appear in the \texttt{make validate} run\par}

BOTH RULES\\ ARE UNSOUND, measured by exhaustive check over all n,m <= 4 and all s: rule 1 has\\ 3374 firing cases and fails in all 3374; rule 2 fires 16832 times and fails in 4462.\\ The cause is G5, not G8: ctr 3 uses the single-Decomp\_devent rule7 shape, so among's\\ count variable n is channelled nowhere, no rule concludes anything about it, and a\\ rule over X alone is sound only if it is a tautology -- among restricts X only\\ JOINTLY with its count. Fixing it means giving among nvalues' N-channel\\ (docs/DECOMP\_FORMAT\_NOTES.md G5), which is a decomposition repair, not a gap.}

\section{How this document was made}\label{sec:howmade}
Every figure, tier, status, verdict and rule below is read from the repository at generation time. Nothing is hand-typed, because a hand-typed number here is a number that will be wrong next week.
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item Tiers: \texttt{python3 tools/mzn\_coverage.py --rank --json}, reached through \texttt{tools/catalog\_index.py}, whose \texttt{ALIAS} table maps a \texttt{cata/} basename to a release global name.
\item Statuses, calibration verdicts and "last measured" lines: the six-row header table at the top of each \texttt{catalog/*.md}, reproduced in full rather than summarised.
\item Rules: \texttt{cata/*.tex}, copied verbatim. This document does not re-render a rule, and the rule count is occurrences of \texttt{\textbackslash{}frac}, never \texttt{grep -c} and never \texttt{wc -l} (\texttt{CLAUDE.md}, ``Traps'').
\item Verdicts: one \texttt{make validate} run, parsed from its own output.
\item The claims paragraphs, the status legend and the calibration vocabulary: \texttt{catalog/README.md}.
\end{itemize}
A verdict is never written as ``correct''. The six status values and the six calibration verdicts below are the only vocabulary used.
\subsection{Status legend}
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.32\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.62\textwidth}@{}}
\hline\endhead
\texttt{validated: sound and minimal at n,m $\in$ \{2,3,4\}} & every generated rule in the entry got \texttt{SOUND and MINIMAL} from \texttt{make validate}, at the sizes \texttt{docs/VALIDATOR.md} enumerates. \textbf{The sizes are the literal set \texttt{\{2,3,4\}} (\texttt{docs/VALIDATOR.md:184}), so \texttt{n = 1} is unchecked} --- the value used to read \texttt{n,m <= 4}, which overstated it; swept 2026-09-22, W1-T19 \\[0.3em]
\texttt{partly validated} & some rules \texttt{SOUND and MINIMAL}, some flagged. The per-rule verdicts are in the entry \\[0.3em]
\texttt{flagged} & every generated rule is flagged: \texttt{UNSOUND}, \texttt{AMBIGUOUS}, \texttt{VACUOUS}, \texttt{UNSOUND(firing)} or \texttt{NOT MINIMAL} \\[0.3em]
\texttt{not validatable} & rules exist in \texttt{cata/} but the validator reports the entry out of scope, with a machine-printed reason (an undefined \texttt{D\_k}, an unparsed index equation, an argument that appears in no atom) \\[0.3em]
\texttt{generated, unvalidated} & rules exist and no verdict has been obtained. Distinct from \texttt{not validatable}: nobody has looked \\[0.3em]
\texttt{nothing generated -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{} blocked on G<n>} & no decomposition is encodable, or the generator emits no rule. The gap number is required, not optional \\[0.3em]
\texttt{encodable today, not encoded} & \textbf{added 2026-09-21.} The current format can already express the decomposition, but nothing in the generator does it, so \textbf{there is no gap to name}. Use this rather than inventing a G-number to satisfy the row above. First cases, found by R5: \texttt{strictly\_increasing} / \texttt{strictly\_decreasing}, a pure parameter shift on the validated \texttt{increasing} / \texttt{decreasing} pair --- \texttt{tplus}/\texttt{tmoin} already exist and the \texttt{Addint} printer case is already exercised \\[0.3em]
\hline
\end{longtable}

\section{Disagreements between computed and written numbers}
Each line is a number this run computed from an artifact next to a number written into a file. Neither side is preferred here; the disagreement is reported so it can be settled by whoever owns the file.
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item inverse\_fn: quotes inverse.md's Status as ``encodable today, not encoded''; that row now reads ``encodable today, not encoded --- no gap blocks this decomposition; no...''
\item sort\_fn: quotes sort.md's Status as ``encodable today, not encoded''; that row now reads ``nothing generated --- blocked on G10 --- and on G9 and G3; see Status....''
\end{itemize}

\section{Remaining entries}
No generated rule, no blocking code in the Status row, and a tier inside scope. Most are the \texttt{\_fn} function variants, whose Status defers to the base entry rather than asserting one.
\subsubsection*{\texttt{disjunctive}}
\label{entry:disjunctive}
\entryfield{Tier}{\textbf{D} --- \texttt{D literature + solver-\allowbreak{}native}, ecode \texttt{E2}}
\entryfield{Status}{\texttt{not validatable}}
\entryfield{Generated}{\textbf{2} rules --- in \texttt{cata/cumulative.tex}, \textbf{not} in a file of this name. See the section below}
\entryfield{Validator}{out of scope. Machine-printed reason: ``the durations d\_i appear as uninterpreted symbols inside index equations (t'=t-d\_i), AND the resource capacity appears in no atom, so the rule is schematic over a parameter the artifact never states''}
\entryfield{Calibration}{\textbf{out of reach} --- by \emph{gaps} (G15, G1), not by structure; and the gap list here is one item shorter than \texttt{cumulative}'s}
\entryfield{Last measured}{2026-09-21, \texttt{make validate}, \texttt{grep -\allowbreak{}o '\textbackslash{}frac' cata/\allowbreak{}cumulative.\allowbreak{}tex \textbackslash{}| wc -\allowbreak{}l}, \texttt{python3 tools/\allowbreak{}mzn\_\allowbreak{}coverage.\allowbreak{}py -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}rank -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}json}}
